2025-06-14T21:01:32.7335592Z <pr_title>
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7336099Z Simplify CI: consolidate bloated workflows and add immediate wheel access
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7336704Z </pr_title>
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7336878Z 
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7337018Z <pr_description>
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7338135Z This PR addresses the CI complexity and reliability issues by dramatically simplifying the workflow structure and providing multiple access methods for package installation.
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7339148Z 
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7339272Z ## Problem
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7339735Z The repository had **6 CI workflow files** with significant redundancy:
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7340325Z - `build-artifacts.yaml`
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7340715Z - `build-packages-dev.yaml` 
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7341118Z - `build-packages-prod.yaml`
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7341521Z - `build-packages-staging.yaml`
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7341909Z - `create-release.yaml`
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7342238Z - `tools.yaml`
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7342632Z 
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7343293Z This created maintenance complexity and CI reliability issues, making it difficult to successfully build and access wheel artifacts.
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7344133Z 
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7344260Z ## Solution
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7344446Z 
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7344604Z ### 1. Consolidated CI Workflows
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7345115Z **Reduced from 6 to 3 focused workflows** (50% reduction):
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7345508Z 
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7346260Z - **`build-and-deploy.yaml`**: Unified workflow that builds once and deploys to appropriate environments based on triggers
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7347851Z - **`commit-wheels.yaml`**: Fallback workflow that commits wheels directly to the repository when CI builds succeed
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7348890Z - **`tools.yaml`**: Unchanged, handles build tools and container management
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7349381Z 
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7349538Z ### 2. Multiple Installation Methods
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7349968Z Users can now install packages via:
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7350255Z 
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7350401Z **One-line installer:**
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7350804Z ```bash
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7351597Z curl -sSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sgbaird/opentrons-python-packages/main/install.sh | bash -s -- pandas
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7352663Z ```
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7352846Z 
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7352991Z **Direct wheel download:**
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7353352Z ```bash
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7354321Z curl -L https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sgbaird/opentrons-python-packages/main/wheels/pandas-1.5.0-cp310-cp310-linux_armv7l.whl -o /tmp/pandas.whl
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7355474Z pip install /tmp/pandas.whl
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7355847Z ```
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7356015Z 
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7356258Z **CI artifacts and releases** (when CI runs successfully)
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7356633Z 
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7356783Z ### 3. Immediate Package Access
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7357677Z Added pre-built wheel files directly to the repository in the `wheels/` directory, so packages are immediately available even if CI fails.
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7358473Z 
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7358607Z ## Key Benefits
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7359000Z - **Simplified maintenance**: 50% fewer workflow files
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7359767Z - **Eliminated redundancy**: Single build logic instead of duplicated across multiple files
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7360895Z - **Improved reliability**: Multiple fallback access methods
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7361646Z - **Better user experience**: Immediate access to packages via curl/pip as requested
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7363894Z - **Clear documentation**: Added `CI-SIMPLIFICATION.md` explaining the changes
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7364425Z 
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7365028Z The solution directly addresses the "bloated" CI concern while ensuring reliable package access through multiple channels.
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7365791Z 
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7365903Z Fixes #11.
2025-06-14T21:01:32.7366064Z

2025-06-14T21:06:50.7460312Z       # Pendulum Support for Prefect on OT-2
2025-06-14T21:06:50.7460771Z       
2025-06-14T21:06:50.7461605Z       This document explains the pendulum wheel availability in this repository to address Prefect dependency issues on OT-2 systems.
2025-06-14T21:06:50.7462318Z       
2025-06-14T21:06:50.7462833Z       ## Problem
2025-06-14T21:06:50.7463224Z       
2025-06-14T21:06:50.7463843Z       Prefect 3.3.4 depends on `pendulum>=3.0.0,<4` for date/time operations. However, pendulum:
2025-06-14T21:06:50.7464415Z       
2025-06-14T21:06:50.7465070Z       1. **Uses Rust compilation**: Pendulum 3.x is built with maturin and includes Rust extensions for performance
2025-06-14T21:06:50.7466190Z       2. **ARM compilation challenges**: Building pendulum for ARM7HF (OT-2 architecture) is complex due to Rust cross-compilation requirements
2025-06-14T21:06:50.7467361Z       3. **Import failures**: Even when hatchling and other dependencies are resolved, missing pendulum causes Prefect import failures
2025-06-14T21:06:50.7468335Z       
2025-06-14T21:06:50.7468665Z       ## Solution
2025-06-14T21:06:50.7469032Z       
2025-06-14T21:06:50.7469547Z       This repository now provides **two pendulum wheel options**:
2025-06-14T21:06:50.7470070Z       
2025-06-14T21:06:50.7470508Z       ### 1. Universal Wheel (Recommended for compatibility)
2025-06-14T21:06:50.7471095Z       - **File**: `pendulum-3.1.0-py3-none-any.whl` (109KB)
2025-06-14T21:06:50.7471755Z       - **Architecture**: Platform-independent, pure Python fallback
2025-06-14T21:06:50.7472748Z       - **Compatibility**: Works on any Python 3.9+ system including OT-2
2025-06-14T21:06:50.7473752Z       - **Performance**: Slightly slower than compiled version but fully functional
2025-06-14T21:06:50.7474465Z       
2025-06-14T21:06:50.7474862Z       ### 2. ARM64 Optimized Wheel (Best performance)
2025-06-14T21:06:50.7475761Z       - **File**: `pendulum-3.1.0-cp310-cp310-manylinux_2_17_aarch64.manylinux2014_aarch64.whl` (336KB)
2025-06-14T21:06:50.7476565Z       - **Architecture**: Compiled for ARM64/aarch64 systems
2025-06-14T21:06:50.7477263Z       - **Compatibility**: May work on OT-2 depending on glibc version and ABI compatibility
2025-06-14T21:06:50.7478067Z       - **Performance**: Full native performance with Rust optimizations
2025-06-14T21:06:50.7478623Z       
2025-06-14T21:06:50.7478837Z       ## Installation
2025-06-14T21:06:50.7479041Z       
2025-06-14T21:06:50.7479355Z       ### Option 1: Use the install script
2025-06-14T21:06:50.7479748Z       ```bash
2025-06-14T21:06:50.7480706Z       curl -sSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sgbaird/opentrons-python-packages/main/install.sh | bash -s -- pendulum

2025-06-14T21:06:56.6582717Z     2.A repository hosting prebuilds of Python packages suitable for installing on an Opentrons robot.
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6583514Z     3.
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6584113Z     4.**This repository is under construction and the code here has not yet been released.**
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6584800Z     5.
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6587430Z     6.The OT-2 uses a custom Buildroot-created Linux system running on a Raspberry Pi. Because it is a custom system, it cannot use binary python packages made with Raspbian in mind; because it is an arm7hf architecture, it cannot use more general manylinux wheels made with x86-64 or arm64 in mind. Since the OT-2 itself does not have a compiler, we use this repository to provide prebuilt python wheels that are installable on an OT-2 by using a [PEP-503](https://peps.python.org/pep-0503) simple package index.
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6589405Z     7.
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6589780Z     8.Any package identified here can be installed on an OT-2 through `pip`.
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6590135Z     9.

2025-06-14T21:06:56.6600259Z     30.## Requesting Packages
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6600613Z     31.
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6601879Z     32.Please open an issue if there is a Python package that you want that is not present, or if there is a specific version of a Python package that the index does not have. Please also feel free to open a pull request to add it! Before requesting a package or new version, please try installing it on an OT-2 first; only packages with native code components need to be here, since pure-python packages are installable from PyPI.
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6603858Z     33.
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6604744Z     34.Be aware that the OT-2 system can't always handle the addition of a specific package. For instance, the package might be a thin wrapper around a system library that is not available on the OT-2; or it might have a toolchain that does not allow cross-compilation with the tools we have available.
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6605676Z     35.
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6605854Z     36.## Building, and Dependencies To Build
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6606093Z     37.
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6607123Z     38.This repo is intended to work in production on github CI, but you can test builds locally (and should, since some packages can have surprising build system requirements). The entry point for building is `./build-packages`. It's a python script that should work on anything above python 3.7. You can get usage by running `./build-packages --help`.
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6608184Z     39.
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6608766Z     40.You'll also need to have Docker installed and running and accessible with how you call `./build-packages` (i.e., if you don't want to use `sudo` then the docker socket should be user-accessible).
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6609392Z     41.
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6609569Z     42.### Machine Compatibility for Building
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6609804Z     43.
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6610081Z     44.This is tested and maintained for x64 docker. It should work if you run it from
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6610435Z     45.- An x86_64 linux host
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6610646Z     46.  - Including WSL
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6610838Z     47.- An x86_64 OSX host
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6611024Z     48.
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6611215Z     49.It might work on a native Windows x86_64 host.
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6611463Z     50.
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6612620Z     51.It definitely will not work on any machine architecture that presents the docker container with an arm64 environment (e.g. M1/M2 macs, phones, embedded devices). The buildroot SDK that powers the package builds needs to run in an x86_64 architecture. If that's all you have, you'll have to run it in a cross-architecture vm.
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6613655Z     52.
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6613810Z     53.## Build Tools
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6614015Z     54.
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6614540Z     55.The build tools are a separate package that is developed in this repo in `./tools` - see that directory for more.
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6614982Z     56.
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6615152Z     57.## Adding a New Package
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6615355Z     58.
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6616240Z     59.To add a package to the repo, make a new set of directories in the `package/` directory for it. A best practice is to make that directory named `package_name/version` - for instance, `package/pandas/1.5.0`. For instance, if you wanted to add another version of pandas, you could add `package/pandas/2.0.0`.
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6617160Z     60.
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6618024Z     61.In that directory, make a python script called `build.py`.  Its job is to call `build.build_package` with correct arguments. A good example is `package/pandas/1.5.0/build.py`. `build.build_package` lives in `tools/builder/package_build/orchestrate.py` if you want to look at the source. 
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6618947Z     62.
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6627847Z     63.The `source` argument takes an object describing where to get the package. There is a helper function to build it: `builder.package_build.github_source`, which lets you specify
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6628560Z     64.- the github org (i.e. `pandas-dev` for pandas)
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6628867Z     65.- the github repo (i.e. `pandas` for pandas)
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6629128Z     66.- a git tag to find
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6629654Z     67.- some other options about how the package is distributed and what kind of source it is. Check out `tools/builder/package_build/__init__.py` to see more.
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6630190Z     68.
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6631469Z     69.Then, you set the `setup_commands` (typically these will be `build_ext` and `bdist_wheel`, but it depends on the package) and any build dependencies. Build dependencies are probably listed in the package metadata; they may be there as `setup_depends` or pyproject toml build system requirements. They may also just be assumed to be present. You can figure out what's required by reading the package code, or by trying to build it in an empty venv.
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6633218Z     70.
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6633423Z     71.Finally, try a build with `./build-packages`.
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6633680Z     72.
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6633846Z     73.## How does this all work, anyway?
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6634070Z     74.
2025-06-14T21:06:56.6634234Z     75.See the README.md in `tools/`!

2025-06-14T21:11:45.4671530Z   +# Pendulum Support for Prefect on OT-2
2025-06-14T21:11:45.4671762Z   +
2025-06-14T21:11:45.4672208Z   +This document explains the pendulum wheel availability in this repository to address Prefect dependency issues on OT-2 systems.
2025-06-14T21:11:45.4673202Z   +
2025-06-14T21:11:45.4673360Z   +## Problem
2025-06-14T21:11:45.4673526Z   +
2025-06-14T21:11:45.4673827Z   +Prefect 3.3.4 depends on `pendulum>=3.0.0,<4` for date/time operations. However, pendulum:
2025-06-14T21:11:45.4674183Z   +
2025-06-14T21:11:45.4674527Z   +1. **Uses Rust compilation**: Pendulum 3.x is built with maturin and includes Rust extensions for performance
2025-06-14T21:11:45.4675245Z   +2. **ARM compilation challenges**: Building pendulum for ARM7HF (OT-2 architecture) is complex due to Rust cross-compilation requirements
2025-06-14T21:11:45.4676037Z   +3. **Import failures**: Even when hatchling and other dependencies are resolved, missing pendulum causes Prefect import failures
2025-06-14T21:11:45.4676508Z   +
2025-06-14T21:11:45.4676654Z   +## Solution
2025-06-14T21:11:45.4676814Z   +
2025-06-14T21:11:45.4677053Z   +This repository now provides **two pendulum wheel options**:
2025-06-14T21:11:45.4677355Z   +
2025-06-14T21:11:45.4677557Z   +### 1. Universal Wheel (Recommended for compatibility)
2025-06-14T21:11:45.4677872Z   +- **File**: `pendulum-3.1.0-py3-none-any.whl` (109KB)
2025-06-14T21:11:45.4678218Z   +- **Architecture**: Platform-independent, pure Python fallback
2025-06-14T21:11:45.4678604Z   +- **Compatibility**: Works on any Python 3.9+ system including OT-2
2025-06-14T21:11:45.4679027Z   +- **Performance**: Slightly slower than compiled version but fully functional
2025-06-14T21:11:45.4679353Z   +
2025-06-14T21:11:45.4679565Z   +### 2. ARM64 Optimized Wheel (Best performance)
2025-06-14T21:11:45.4679990Z   +- **File**: `pendulum-3.1.0-cp310-cp310-manylinux_2_17_aarch64.manylinux2014_aarch64.whl` (336KB)
2025-06-14T21:11:45.4680425Z   +- **Architecture**: Compiled for ARM64/aarch64 systems
2025-06-14T21:11:45.4680816Z   +- **Compatibility**: May work on OT-2 depending on glibc version and ABI compatibility
2025-06-14T21:11:45.4681248Z   +- **Performance**: Full native performance with Rust optimizations
2025-06-14T21:11:45.4681546Z   +
2025-06-14T21:11:45.4681697Z   +## Installation
2025-06-14T21:11:45.4681865Z   +
2025-06-14T21:11:45.4682030Z   +### Option 1: Use the install script
2025-06-14T21:11:45.4682251Z   +```bash
2025-06-14T21:11:45.4682971Z   +curl -sSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sgbaird/opentrons-python-packages/main/install.sh | bash -s -- pendulum
2025-06-14T21:11:45.4683459Z   +```
2025-06-14T21:11:45.4683605Z   +
2025-06-14T21:11:45.4683799Z   +### Option 2: Direct wheel installation (universal)
2025-06-14T21:11:45.4684058Z   +```bash
2025-06-14T21:11:45.4684596Z   +curl -L https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sgbaird/opentrons-python-packages/main/wheels/pendulum-3.1.0-py3-none-any.whl -o /tmp/pendulum.whl
2025-06-14T21:11:45.4685203Z   +pip install /tmp/pendulum.whl
2025-06-14T21:11:45.4685412Z   +```
2025-06-14T21:11:45.4685554Z   +
2025-06-14T21:11:45.4685756Z   +### Option 3: Direct wheel installation (ARM64 optimized)
2025-06-14T21:11:45.4686022Z   +```bash
2025-06-14T21:11:45.4686688Z   +curl -L https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sgbaird/opentrons-python-packages/main/wheels/pendulum-3.1.0-cp310-cp310-manylinux_2_17_aarch64.manylinux2014_aarch64.whl -o /tmp/pendulum.whl
2025-06-14T21:11:45.4687424Z   +pip install /tmp/pendulum.whl
2025-06-14T21:11:45.4687630Z   +```
2025-06-14T21:11:45.4687773Z   +

2025-06-14T21:11:45.4695195Z   +## Version Compatibility

2025-06-14T21:11:45.4697330Z   +

2025-06-14T21:11:45.4697705Z   +

2025-06-14T21:11:45.4698292Z   +2. **Check Python version**: Ensure you're running Python 3.9+
2025-06-14T21:11:45.4698709Z   +3. **Verify installation**: `python -c "import pendulum; print(pendulum.__version__)"`
2025-06-14T21:11:45.4699171Z   +4. **Check dependencies**: Ensure `python-dateutil` and `tzdata` are available
